# M0 gate summary

- **For:** the product owner, ahead of the M0 exit review (plan Sections 21.1, 21.2, 22.4 and 23.3).
- **State:** agent-written. It records your decisions of 22 October 2026, all 128 items of the M0 decision page (Sections 1, 3 and 4), your answers of 23 October 2026 to the five points they left open (Section 1, item 4; as relayed, `evidence/M0-Decisions/followup/owner-answers-2026-10-23.md`) and your decision of 24 October 2026 on device A (Section 4.3, D-1), the only things in it you signed or decided, and it cites your first device results, from 25 October 2026 UTC, as you recorded them (Section 1, item 2); no sign-off of a plan 23.3 role is recorded. Its inputs were agent-reviewed as merged; of the agent edits since 19 October 2026, an agent review accepted the M0 exit preparation, the device-A records of the update of 24 October 2026 and the fixes of your first runs' failures (folded in on 27 October 2026), with minor findings, as relayed; the others, this summary's integration of 26 October 2026 and that fold-in included, have had no accepting review (Sections 6.2 and 8). Human sign-off pending on everything below except your decisions. (This line's history: Section 6.2, and Section 6.3, items 88, 178 and 215.)
- **Evidence paths** are relative to `Docs/Stage0/` unless they start with a repository folder. **Dates.** A run or an event (a CI run, Xcode's update) is dated by UTC, with its time where known, and with the build Mac's date beside it wherever that differs: the build Mac's clock (NZDT, UTC+13) reads the next day from 11:00 UTC. The rounds of work and the dated notes of the documents ("until 20 October 2026 this line said ...") carry the build Mac's date, as their commits do: the fix's second round worked on 19 October 2026 UTC, which its notes date 20 October.
- **Versions, branch, sources and this summary's own checks:** Section 8 (moved there from this header on 20 October 2026: M0 final review round 3, R7-09).

Plan 21.2 adds the threat model, an interaction prototype, the dependency lock, the ADRs and the `SylviaDesign` v0.1 package.

### 2.10 The CI from a clean tree, and since

**On 14 October 2026 UTC (15 October on the build Mac's clock), under Xcode 27.0**, `Docs/Stage0/evidence/M0-Integration/commands/ci-assembled.sh` ran `Scripts/ci.sh` twice on the assembled code, every drop-in on the simulator `Sylvia-M0-assemble` (iPhone 17, iOS 26.5), with `G02_LOOPBACK_ONLY=1` and every player at test volume 0.01, so every audio measurement in these runs was taken at test volume (integration log 7.6). Each run was to start from a clean tree (every `.build` and DerivedData deleted, then `Scripts/fixtures/fetch.sh` and the cache generators), but the wrapper logs no deletion, and the fixture check is logged for the first run only (`evidence/M0-Integration/prep-fixtures-assembled.log`), so for the second run both are unlogged observations (integration log 7.6). Both used Xcode 27.0 (27A266a), which the same runs' committed logs name (`evidence/M0-Integration/ci-full-clean-run-assembled-first.log` lines 58-59, `evidence/M0-Integration/ci-second-run-71-g05-recovery.log` lines 3-4), and `12-dependency-lock`'s toolchain check, which passes only with that build, passed in both. (Until 20 October 2026 this paragraph said both runs started from a deleted tree with the fixtures checked, and that their Xcode was inferred because their logs name none: final review round 2, R6-03 and evidence R2-04.)

- **The second round's complete run (19 October 2026, 13:08-15:04 UTC; 20 October on the build Mac's clock), from a fresh clone under Xcode 27.1.** `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/commands/ci-final-clone.sh` cloned the repository again (`git clone --no-local`, into `.build/scratch/final-clone-2`), set up Git LFS there, checked the fixtures and ran `Scripts/ci.sh` on `87d9dde`, the round's code, with no uncommitted file, on this agent's simulator `Sylvia-M0-final-fix`, with `G02_LOOPBACK_ONLY=1` and every player at test volume 0.01 (`evidence/M0-Integration/ci-full-clean-run-final.log`, which also records the screen lock state and the default audio output at its start and end; every drop-in's log in `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/ci-logs/`; the one-minute load, 1.6-50.5, and the booted simulators each minute, never more than one, in `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/ci-full-clean-run-final-load.tsv`). **11 of the 14 drop-ins passed**, the same eleven as in the first round: `00-git-lfs`, `10-contracts` (18 schemas, 657 fixtures), `11-capture-anchor-contracts` (with the A3 check at 10,000 segments), `13-hard-rules` (with the new trust and TLS patterns: 0 findings, 49 allowed), `20-design`, `21-design-catalog`, `31-g01-epub`, `32-g01-pdf`, `40-g02-transfer` (the guards' new patterns, and the queue entry's acknowledgement captured: 15 captures, 10 of 10 schemas), `50-g03-intake` and `70-g05-ai`. Three stopped on the build Mac's state, as expected: `12-dependency-lock` on its toolchain check alone; `60-g04-audio` at its new audio output guard, 2 s in, before it built the spike or ran a test, because the default output was AirPlay with a presentation latency of 2.012 s, above the 0.3 s it accepts (the built-in speakers, 0.027 s, were only the alert output); and `71-g05-recovery` at its row-10 guard after its 73 host tests, because the screen was locked (at the start and at the end of the run). Both of the last two passed whole when run alone, from clean, later that day (next two bullets).
- **G0.5 recovery on the current code, with its class-B readback (19 October 2026, 15:14-15:20 UTC; 20 October, 04:14-04:20, on the build Mac's clock).** `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/commands/ci-71-allow-missing-class-b.sh` ran the drop-in alone from clean on `c4fbfbd` (the same recovery code as `87d9dde`) with `G05_ALLOW_MISSING_CLASS_B=1`, which turns its row-10 guard's stop into a warning, because every earlier run of this code had the screen locked. This time the screen was unlocked at the start and at the end (no agent unlocks it), so the class-B tests ran and the guard found its evidence: class B read back on 53 files and directories, the SQLite files class B ("row 10 evidence present"). Every step passed: 73 host tests, the crash drills (900 and 150 iterations, 0 failures), the save latency (p95 1.3 ms; budget 200 ms; indicative), the restore drill (49 checks), the restore crash and migration put-back drills (300 and 240 iterations), the backup-manifest contract check, 68 simulator tests and the iOS device build (`evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/ci-71-g05-recovery-locked.log`, named before the run for the locked screen of the earlier runs, like its script: the run found the screen unlocked and did not need the relaxed guard, `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/README.md`; step logs in `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/g05-recovery/`). So report row 10's class-B evidence, runbook Session 0.8's, is current for the recovery code, which has not changed since (Section 5.1). (Until 20 October 2026 this section said that the drop-in's simulator tests needed the unlocked screen too: final review round 2, CC2-02. Until the review of the decisions round on 22 October 2026 it said that Session 0.8 was done for the current code, although the drop-in had gained the lock harness's steps that day, which no run of the whole drop-in had then passed, so 0.8 was to run it once more (the bullet on G0.5 recovery with the lock harness, below, where that run, on `9cdb0a02`, passed): its findings HON-6 and CHK-07.)
- **G0.4 with the built-in output (19 October 2026, 15:24-16:53 UTC; 20 October, 04:24-05:53, on the build Mac's clock).** When it began, the Mac's default output was the built-in speakers (0.027 s; no agent changed it), so `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/commands/ci-60-g04-audio-clone.sh` ran the drop-in alone, from clean, in the complete run's clone at `87d9dde`, on `Sylvia-M0-final-fix`, every player at test volume 0.01. **It passed every step** (5,334 s): the signed device build, the 155 simulator tests (4 known issues, each a start/resume budget miss for long files on precise timing, of the kind G0.4's report records in rows 21 and 33), the process-kill trial, the excerpt portability check and the quick-capture runs through the Shortcuts app and Control Center, 41 result tables (`evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/ci-60-g04-audio-clone.log`; in `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/g04-audio/` the drop-in's log without the harness's own system-log lines, the text result tables, and `host-load.tsv`, which records the load and the default output every 30 seconds: the built-in speakers in every sample). The seven tests that had failed with the AirPlay output, in the first round's complete run (19 October 2026, 05:03-06:21 UTC; next bullet), passed: the player's clock advanced 1.000, 2.000 and 3.000 s of media per wall second at 1x, 2x and 3x (`evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/g04-audio/results/audio-output.txt`), as in the gate's committed table (the first round's run, with AirPlay, had 0.53-0.59 at 1x), and the soak's tap led the clock by 0.74-0.83 s at 3x (`evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/g04-audio/results/soak.txt`; 6.7-6.8 s with AirPlay). - **The M0 final fix's complete run (19 October 2026, 03:10-06:21 UTC; 16:10-19:21 the same day on the build Mac's clock), from a fresh clone under Xcode 27.1.** `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix/commands/ci-final-clone.sh` cloned the repository (`git clone --no-local`) into `.build/scratch/final-clone`, a path holding `/.build/` (the case of final review CC-01), set up Git LFS there, checked the fixtures and ran `Scripts/ci.sh` on `e3c8659`, the fix's code, with no uncommitted file: this agent's simulator `Sylvia-M0-final-fix` (iPhone 17, iOS 26.5), `G02_LOOPBACK_ONLY=1`, every player at test volume 0.01 (`evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix/ci-full-clean-run-final.log`, moved there on 20 October 2026; every drop-in's log in `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix/ci-logs/`; the one-minute load, 1.1-40.6, and the booted simulators each minute, never more than one, in `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix/ci-full-clean-run-final-load.tsv`). **11 of the 14 drop-ins passed**: `00-git-lfs`, `10-contracts`, `11-capture-anchor-contracts`, `13-hard-rules`, `20-design`, `21-design-catalog`, `31-g01-epub`, `32-g01-pdf`, `40-g02-transfer` (its new trust guards from the `/.build/` path), `50-g03-intake` (the stall tests on the manual clock) and `70-g05-ai`. **Your direction of 15 October 2026 (plan draft 0.6).** M3 keeps the selected passage and its source in view while a note is written and returns to the reading position; M5 adds Explain and Simplify question starters inside Ask, follow-ups in the same source-linked conversation and previews of a citation's retained evidence; a browser-based reader is a separate post-V1 workstream; native iOS/iPadOS V1 and the Mac companion stay first (plan 1.1, 4.1, 13.1, 21.2, 24; acceptance cases T55-T57). It is an approved product direction. It changes none of D01-D17, signs none of the changes above, and approves none of the pending amendments (`M0-plan-amendments.md` front matter); your decisions of 22 October 2026 did. Its new labels, such as Explain and Simplify, go through the same vocabulary process as the AI status phrases the amendments propose (A45, PO-28): design review, a fixture update and a snapshot re-baseline (plan 4.4.9; plan 13.1 says the same of the new labels and prompt templates). Gloss itself is a design reference, not a dependency: the plan records that the snapshot it was read at declares no reuse license, and ADR-0014's inventory now lists it so (`Docs/Licenses/third-party-inventory.md`, "Design references").

**The G0.6 interaction prototype predates that direction.** Its capture sheet shows the quotation once, at the top of a sheet that scrolls, with nothing that keeps the passage in view while the note is written (`Packages/Design/Sources/SylviaDesign/Components/CaptureSheet.swift`; `Apps/DesignCatalog/Sources/App/Layout.swift`, `SheetPage`); and on iPad its side pane is the Thoughts pane, while Ask opens as a page pushed in the reader (`Apps/DesignCatalog/Sources/Prototype/ReaderScreens.swift`, `Apps/DesignCatalog/Sources/Prototype/AskScreen.swift`), with no side-pane discussion. That is design input for M3 and M5, which build these interactions with T55-T57, not an M0 defect: row 72 stands. (Read from the committed sources; nothing was run for this.)

Each table's last column gives the decision; the options and recommended defaults stay as they were written, for the record. Three parts had no recommended default and stayed open: PO-12's sources file and import ledger, which your answers of 23 October 2026 decided (set aside and recovered), and PO-42, which stayed with counsel until that day, when you took it on for now with PO-41, PO-43 and PO-44, a lawyer's review coming before any public App Store release; PO-42's own question, which no default answers, stays open for that review (the last column gives both; Section 1, item 4). (Until the follow-up's second fix, 23 October 2026, this sentence had all three parts open only until your answers: the review of the follow-up, its finding HON4-05.) Where you choose another option, the plan text tagged in the amendments changes. Every question has a recommended default, except PO-12's sources file and import ledger and PO-42, which say why they have none, and every option says what it leads to: in a few words in the tables below, in full in the amendments' Section 4 and the runbook's Section 6. ### 4.1 From the plan amendments (PO-01 to PO-44)

| ID | Question | Options, and what each leads to (short) | Recommended default | Decided (22 October 2026) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PO-01 | Fixed-layout EPUB in V1? | (a) out: such books are refused with a specific message; (b) in, after its own reader spike: a second EPUB layout, untested in Stage 0, joins V1's scope | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-02 | Accept the EPUB compatibility subset and the spike's limits? | (a) accept as configurable defaults, rechecked against more real books in M3; (b) relax some: more crash and time risk; (c) tighten some: real books near a limit are refused (all 195 sampled fit today) | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-03 | Quotation continuing into the next chapter in the V1 UI? | (a) an "extend into next chapter" action: its design and test work join V1; (b) storage only: each selection stays inside one chapter, a narrowing no failed spike forces | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-04 | Keep the reading position by text through settings changes? | (a) return to the text first in view: an adapter change with a test in M3; (b) Readium's progression: the place can move when you change the font or spacing | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-05 | PDFs that forbid copying | (a) read only, no quotation, search or AI text: a narrowing of plan 9.2's supported experience; (b) personal quotation and search, but no AI text; (c) every text feature, as for other PDFs with text | (a) (a narrowing no failed spike forces) | As recommended |
| PO-06 | Password-protected PDFs out of V1? | (a) excluded: such a PDF cannot be read in Sylvia (the spike's tested behaviour); (b) prompt: you can read your own protected PDFs, but Stage 0 built and tested no password flow, so M3 would design and test one, and the copy permission would still decide the text features (PO-05) | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-07 | Tailscale route (with security) | (a) scoped ATS exception: the pin stays the only trust decision, but, as ADR-0008 states, `NSExceptionAllowsInsecureHTTPLoads` also permits cleartext HTTP to that domain, so ATS no longer stops a programming mistake from sending cleartext there, and the exception covers every tailnet name, not only the user's Mac; the client's own HTTPS-only rule is then the guard; (b) publicly trusted MagicDNS certificate (untested; publishes the machine name in Certificate Transparency logs); (c) no route, XFER-04 narrowed | (a) if security approves, otherwise (c); decided at the M0 gate (D16) | As recommended: (a) if your security sign-off approves it after G0.2 device steps 6 and 23, otherwise (c); pending |
| PO-08 | Background transfer promise | (a) "continues when iOS allows it, resumes from verified parts": what the simulator evidence supports; (b) uninterrupted: a promise the simulator evidence and invariant I-10 contradict | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-09 | Part size and task window | keep 16 MiB parts, two connections and four tasks per job: the values the spike measured; or change them: the new values stay unmeasured until the transfer tests run again | keep, tune after the device run (the bound across jobs is PO-38) | As recommended |
| PO-10 | Invitation lifetime and pairing limits (with security) | (a) 10 minutes with per-invitation and per-address limits: guessing stays bounded by the 256-bit single-use secret, destroyed after five wrong attempts; (b) also a global cap: it bounds guessing across addresses too, but anyone on the network who reaches it blocks your own pairing until it resets | (a) | As recommended |
| PO-11 | The `sylvia://` pairing link (with security) | (a) drop it: you pair by the copied code, or a QR code scanned in Sylvia; (b) keep it: another app can claim the scheme and receive the pin and the secret | (a) | As recommended |

### 5.3 Git LFS: decided and implemented

- **Decision.** "Adopt Git LFS for the snapshot and evidence images once the current integration is complete. Use repository-local configuration, update CI and setup instructions, and verify a clean checkout. Leave existing history intact." (product owner, 13 October 2026).
- **Implemented on 19 October 2026** (integration log section 9; ADR-0002, "Binary test baselines and evidence images"). The adoption commit `adab8e2` (parent `504fac2`) adds `.gitattributes` and turns exactly the 777 matched images into LFS pointers: the 527 snapshot references (37,703,582 bytes) and the 250 Stage 0 evidence images (29,875,981 bytes), 67,579,563 bytes in all, held in 719 distinct LFS objects (65,110,465 bytes). The 16 patterns are `**/__Snapshots__/**/*` and `Docs/Stage0/evidence/**/*` for png, jpg, jpeg, gif, heic, tif, tiff and webp, each extension matched in any letter case, each `filter=lfs diff=lfs merge=lfs -text`. The configuration is repository-local (`git lfs install --local`; nothing in your global or the system configuration). CI: `Scripts/ci.d/00-git-lfs.sh`, which `Scripts/ci.sh` runs first in every run, and `lfs: true` in the workflow. Setup: `Scripts/setup-repo.sh` and `CLAUDE.md`'s Setup section. A clean checkout was verified with clones of the local repository (no network). History was left intact: nothing was rewritten, so the image versions committed before the adoption stay in git history as they were.
- **Verification.** An independent agent verification ended, as relayed to this summary's refresh, with the verdict accept-with-minor and no open serious finding; no review record of that verdict is committed, and integration log 9.6 records only its three findings (final review R1-06). Its three minor findings, R3-01 (the wording of a shared-object count), R3-02 (documents that still described LFS as a future step) and R3-03 (the `.gitattributes` comment on letter case), were fixed on 19 October 2026 (`75b312f` to `9ac7789`) and the checks run again (integration log 9.6); those fixes await the next agent review. Human sign-off pending.
- **This refresh's own check** (`evidence/M0-Integration/gate-refresh-checks.log`, part 3): `.gitattributes` present with the 16 patterns, the filter in the repository's own configuration, 777 files listed by `git lfs ls-files`, and `00-git-lfs` passing (part 6).
- **Pushing is your action, and it costs LFS quota.** No agent pushes. *(22 October 2026: pull request #5 merged `m0-integrate` into `main`, merge commit `4c73da57`, so the push it needed has been made; what that push uploaded was not checked, since the records of your decisions used no network.)* The first push of `m0-integrate` uploads the 719 LFS objects (65,110,465 bytes, about 0.061 GiB) through git-lfs's `pre-push` hook to your GitHub account's LFS storage for `jevidon/sylvia`. ADR-0002 quotes GitHub's documentation (read 19 October 2026): GitHub Free and Pro include 10 GiB of LFS storage and 10 GiB of bandwidth, storage counts every LFS object the repository has, and each workflow run with `lfs: true` and each clone that downloads the LFS files (65.1 MB at adoption) uses bandwidth. Which plan this account is on was not checked.
- **Not done yet**, as far as the repository records (not checked on 22 October 2026): the GitHub workflow has not run with `lfs: true`, and no clone was made from GitHub. The patterns cover images only: MP4 or MOV evidence from the device sessions goes into plain git unless you have the patterns extended first (runbook D-8).

- **The technical lead, which is you: two decisions the device fixes left, decided on 27 October 2026: F-1 fixed (`protectionClass(of:)` reads the class afresh), S5 recorded for M1's restore work and not fixed in the spike.** As they were put to you, (their requests 5 and 15; listed here since 27 October 2026, when `m0-device-fixes` was folded into `m0-records-update`: Section 6.3, item 220). **Finding F-1:** `FileProtector.verify` reads a file's class through the same `URL` value that `protect` read before `setAttributes`, and a `URL` caches what it has read, so `protect` reports a mismatch even when the change took effect (on the phone inferred from 8 failing tests whose later opens passed the class check; on the Mac pending until the host-facts probe of `evidence/M0-DeviceFixes/README.md` 7.2, item 3, runs with the screen unlocked). The proposed change: in `verify`, `var fresh = url; fresh.removeAllCachedResourceValues(); let actual = try protectionClass(of: fresh)`; it would correct `DataProtectionTests.mismatchDetected`'s reading and then the records that say an existing file's class cannot be changed (`G05-Recovery.md` row 10 and F3, ADR-0012's paragraph on copied files and its class-B evidence list, and two code comments), whose lines "refused … the class is fixed at creation here" most likely rest on a stale reading until then. **Finding S5:** `FileProtector.copyFile` opens a bundle's file with `O_NOFOLLOW`, which covers its last component only, so a folder above the file swapped for a link after the restore's checks is followed, the copy still having to match the manifest's digest. The proposed change: `copyVerified` opens the validated bundle root once and each listed file with `openat(rootFD, entry.path, O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW_ANY | O_NONBLOCK | O_CLOEXEC)`; it would correct that, refusing a link anywhere in the file's path below the bundle, and needs the host suite, the restore drills and runbook 1.2 on a phone after it, so if you choose it, decide before 1.2 runs again (`evidence/M0-DeviceFixes/fix-1/security/README.md` Section 9). Each is set out in `G05-Recovery.md`, under its open questions (its decisions pending for the technical lead), and in the runbook's technical-lead table; neither blocks your next steps.

125. **A version-specific manifest, and names in another letter case** (SEC-ZIPGUARD-03, major): ADR-0014's rule 5 said that a product `Package.swift`, which every build evaluates again in its own environment, reads nothing but its own text, and its section 5, on which your security sign-off of its item 4 rests, that CI reads every value a product manifest gives as the build uses it; but SwiftPM evaluates a version-specific manifest (`Package@swift-6.4.swift`) instead of the `Package.swift` beside it, the guard's manifest-input rule read the text of `Package.swift` alone, and nothing read the other file, so it could read the environment and give a build a source path, a flag or a test-only package that the check, run without the variable as CI runs it, never saw. ADR-0014's list of what the check cannot see did not name it, though row 76 and item 113 named the route as open. The fix, an agent's change to `Scripts/check-dependency-lock.py` (`4d72eeca`), fails any file under the product roots whose name begins with `Package@` and ends with `.swift`, in any letter case, since SwiftPM matched `PACKAGE@SWIFT-6.4.SWIFT` too. Reading the other names the guard reads as it spells them found routes of the same kind on the default macOS file system, which ignores letter case: git checks a path tracked under `packages/` out into `Packages/`, where SwiftPM compiles it, SwiftPM and XcodeGen load `package.swift` and `Project.yml`, and clang loads `MODULE.MODULEMAP` and `module_private.map`, none of which the guard read. It now fails a root or a manifest named in another letter case and reads module maps by every name clang reads them by. Nine routes, planted in copies of the tree at `fcbd6c89`, passed the check before the change and fail it after, and four controls fail both times (`evidence/M0-ExitPrep/fix-2/reproductions.log`, part 1); the tools' behaviour is in its part 2.
